107.47128pt\raise-94.18748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\gamma_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.02527pt\raise-89.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.31845pt\raise-0.94794pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.31845pt\raise 0.94794pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.55919pt\raise-53.94446pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{ij}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.70471pt\raise-37.31764pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.31845pt\raise-0.94794pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.31845pt\raise 0.94794pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.31845pt\raise-0.94794pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.31845pt\raise 0.94794pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.61945pt\raise-0.78503pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.61945pt\raise 0.78503pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.1901pt\raise-50.6111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{ik}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.6289pt\raise-7.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.61945pt\raise-0.78503pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.61945pt\raise 0.78503pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern-0.61945pt\raise-0.78503pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern 0.61945pt\raise 0.78503pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

A 2-morphism in
\holim 2\textsf{VectBd}(U(M)_{\bullet})
from
(\tilde{\gamma}_{i},\tilde{\phi}_{ij})
to
(\gamma_{i},\phi_{ij})
consists of

•

\phi_{i}\in 2\textsf{VectBd}(\sqcup_{ij}U_{ij})_{2}
from
\tilde{\gamma}_{i}
to
\gamma_{i}
;

•

a coherence condition held on
U_{ij}
,

\phi_{ij}\circ\phi_{i}=\phi_{j}\circ\tilde{\phi}_{ij}.

Then the
(3,1)
-sheaf
2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}:\mathsf{Mfd}^{op}\to 2\textsf{Gpd}
consists of

•

2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}(M)_{0}
: an object is a pair
(\{U_{i}\},\mathcal{E})
, where
\{U_{i}\}
is an open cover of
M
and
\mathcal{E}
is an element in
\holim 2\textsf{VectBd}(U(M)_{\bullet})_{0}
;

•

2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}(M)_{1}
: a 1-morphism between
(\{U_{i}\},\mathcal{E})
and
(\{\tilde{U}_{i}\},\tilde{\mathcal{E}})
is a pair consisting of a common refinement
\{V_{i}\}
of
\{U_{i}\}
and
\{\tilde{U}_{i}\}
and an element
\alpha\in\holim 2\textsf{VectBd}(V(M)_{\bullet})_{1}
;

•

2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}(M)_{2}
: a 2-morphism between
(\{V_{i}\},\alpha)
and
(\{\tilde{V}_{i}\},\tilde{\alpha})
consists of a common refinement
\{W_{i}\}
of
\{V_{i}\}
and
\{\tilde{V}_{i}\}
and an element
\beta\in\holim 2\textsf{VectBd}(W(M)_{\bullet})_{2}
. Moreover,
(\{W_{i}\},\beta)
and
(\{\tilde{W}_{i}\},\tilde{\beta})
are identified if
\beta
and
\tilde{\beta}
agree on a further common refinement of
\{W_{i}\}
and
\{\tilde{W}_{i}\}
.

Thus, with the plus construction in
[
NS11
]
, we describe what are the 2-vector bundles on manifolds, and their 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms. All is organised in the above (3,1)-sheaf
2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}
. We notice that there are also other similar methods towards 2-bundles
[
Bar
, Section 2.3.1]
.

4.2
Principal
\mathcal{G}
-bundle and the associated 2-vector bundles

In this section we construct a model of principal 2-bundles and the 2-vector bundles associated to it.

Let
\mathcal{G}=G_{1}\Rightarrow G_{0}
be a Lie 2-group. The
(3,1)
-presheaf,
B\mathcal{G}:\mathsf{Mfd}^{op}\to 2\textsf{Gpd}
is constructed as following: first of all,
B\mathcal{G}(U)
is a 2-groupoid made up by the following data:

•

B\mathcal{G}(U)_{0}
: an object is
U\times\mathcal{G}
;

•

B\mathcal{G}(U)_{1}
: a 1-simplex is a groupoid morphism
g_{01}:U\to\mathcal{G}
, which is simply a morphism
U\to G_{0}
because
U
is a manifold;

•

B\mathcal{G}(U)_{2}
: a 2-simplex with edges
g_{ij}
for
0\leq i<j\leq 2
, is given by a 2-morphism
f:U\to G_{1}
from
g_{02}
to
g_{01}\cdot g_{12}
.

Given a morphism
U\xrightarrow{\psi}V
, the associated functor
B\mathcal{G}(V)\to B\mathcal{G}(U)
is given by pre-composition.
Then with plus construction we arrive at a
(3,1)
-sheaf
B\mathcal{G}^{+}:\mathsf{Mfd}^{op}\to 2\textsf{Gpd}
, which organises the information of
\mathcal{G}
-principal bundles, their morphisms and 2-morphisms together.

Then with a 2-representation of
\mathcal{G}
on 2
Vect
,
(F,\eta,\phi,\psi)
, we form a functor
A:B\mathcal{G}\to 2\textsf{VectBd}
, which is the process analogue to associate a
G
-principal bundle to an associated vector bundle via a representation of
G
. We call
A
the 2-associating functor, and the result the associated 2-bundles.

It is relative easy to do this:

•

A_{0}:B\mathcal{G}(U)_{0}\to 2\textsf{VectBd}(U)_{0},\quad U\times\mathcal{G}\mapsto U\times\textsf{Rep}^{\mathcal{G}}
;

•

A_{1}:B\mathcal{G}(U)_{1}\to 2\textsf{VectBd}(U)_{1},\quad g_{01}\mapsto\gamma_{01}:=F\circ g_{01}
;

•

A_{2}:B\mathcal{G}(U)_{2}\to 2\textsf{VectBd}(U)_{2},\quad f\mapsto\phi:=\eta\circ f
.

Then, by the functoriality of the plus construction, we get the 2-associating functor
A^{+}:B\mathcal{G}^{+}\to 2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}
.

Remark 4.2
.

5
A model of Equivariant 2-vector bundles

In this section we give the construction of a model of equivariant 2-vector bundle. And we show that (nonequivariant) 2-vector bundles and principal
\mathcal{G}-
bundle with
\mathcal{G}
a coherent Lie 2-group are examples of equivariant 2-vector bundles.

5.1
Groupoid action on a category

Before we construct equivariant 2-vector bundles, we define the action of a topological groupoid on a topological category.

Example 5.1

(Groupoid action on a category)
.

In
[
MM03
, Section 5.3, page 126]
there is a definition of a right action of a Lie groupoid on another Lie groupoid. This definition can be generalized to that of a right action of a topological groupoid
\Gamma
on a topological category
A
. It is given by two right actions of
\Gamma
on the space of morphisms
A_{1}
and on the space of objects
A_{0}
, i.e. for
i=1
,
2
, the continuous maps

\epsilon_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad\mu_{i}:A_{i}\times_{\epsilon_{i},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow A_{i}.

(5.75)

which satisfies the following identities:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}\circ s=\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{0}\circ t,

(5.76)

\displaystyle(a_{i}\gamma)\gamma^{\prime}=a_{i}(\gamma\gamma^{\prime})

(5.77)

\displaystyle a_{i}1_{\epsilon_{i}(a_{i})}=a_{i}

(5.78)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{i}(a_{i}\gamma)=s(\gamma).

(5.79)

for any
a_{i}\in A_{i}
and
\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma_{i}
with
i=1,2
.
We write
a_{i}\gamma
for
\mu_{i}(a_{i},\gamma)
with
a_{i}\in A_{i}
.

For each
x\in\Gamma_{0}
, the fibre
A_{x}=\epsilon^{-1}_{1}(x)
is a full subcategory of
A
with objects
\epsilon^{-1}_{0}(x)
. So we get a family of subcategories indexed by the points
x\in\Gamma_{0}
. If
A
is a groupoid, the groupoid
\Gamma
acts on this family by a groupoid isomorphism
A_{x}\rightarrow A_{x^{\prime}}
for each arrow
x^{\prime}\rightarrow x
in
\Gamma_{1}
. However, if
A
is not a groupoid and
\Gamma
contains not only the identity morphisms, there is no well-defined action by
\Gamma
on the family.

Remark 5.2
.

There are several examples of the definition of right action of a Lie groupoid on a topological category in Example
5.1
.
The construction of a right action of a Lie groupoid on another Lie groupoid and that on a smooth manifold in
[
MM03
, Section 5.3, page 126]
are
definitely examples for it.

Another example is the action of a groupoid
\Gamma
on a vector space
V
given in
[
MRW17
, Section 4]
, which can be viewed as a functor

F
from
\Gamma
to the category
Vect
of vector spaces. Each object
x
in
\Gamma_{0}
is mapped to a vector space
V_{x}
and each arrow
x\xrightarrow{a}y
is mapped to a linear map
V_{x}\xrightarrow{F(a)}V_{y}
. This gives an action of
\Gamma
on the disjoint union

A=\coprod_{x\in\Gamma_{0}}V_{x},

whose morphisms are the
k
-linear maps from a vector space
V_{x}
to itself, with

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}:A_{0}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad(v_{x}\in V_{x})\mapsto x

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}:A_{1}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad(f:V_{x}\mapsto V_{x})\mapsto x

\displaystyle\mu_{0}:A_{0}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow A_{0},\quad(v_{x},\gamma_{1})\mapsto s(\gamma_{1})

\displaystyle\mu_{1}:A_{1}\times_{\epsilon_{1},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow A_{1},\quad((f:V_{x}\mapsto V_{x}),\gamma_{1})\mapsto\gamma_{1}^{-1}\circ f\circ\gamma_{1}

where
\gamma_{1}^{-1}\circ f\circ\gamma_{1}
is the composition of
k
-linear maps.

Example 5.3

(Groupoid equivariant maps)
.

Let
A^{1}
and
A^{2}
denote two topological categories with a right action of the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
. A
\Gamma
-equivariant map
f:A^{1}\rightarrow A^{2}

between them is a functor commuting with the structure maps

\epsilon^{j}_{i}:A_{i}^{j}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad\mu_{i}^{j}:A_{i}^{j}\times_{\epsilon^{j}_{i},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow A^{j}_{i}

with
j=1,2
and
i=1,2
.
Explicitly,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}_{i}\circ f

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{1}_{i}

(5.80)

\displaystyle f(a^{1}_{i}g)

\displaystyle=f(a^{1}_{i})g

(5.81)

for
i=1
,
2
and
g\in\Gamma_{1}
with
\epsilon^{1}_{i}(a^{1}_{i})=t(g)
. Note that (
5.80
) implies
\epsilon^{2}_{i}(f(a^{1}_{i}))=t(g)
.

Let
Fun_{\Gamma}(A^{1},A^{2})
denote the full subcategory of the functor category
Fun(A^{1},A^{2})

whose objects are the
\Gamma
-equivariant maps between
A^{1}
and
A^{2}
.

The rest part of this section will not be used in the construction of equivariant 2-vector bundles. We keep this part here for completeness of the discussion.

Example 5.4
.

We know that if we have a
G
-space
X
, then a group homomorphism
\phi:H\rightarrow G
induces an
H
-action on
X
. A natural question is: given an action of a topological groupoid
\Gamma
on a topological category
A
defined in Examples
5.1
and a groupoid homomorphism

\beta:\Gamma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Gamma
, can we define an action of
\Gamma^{\prime}
on
A
?

A quick answer is below.

If
A
has a right action of
\Gamma
on it and
\beta:\Gamma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Gamma
is a topological groupoid homomorphism which is injective on the object level, then there are well-defined maps
\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}
such that
\epsilon_{0}:=\beta_{0}\circ\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}:=\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}\circ s=\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}\circ t
.

\beta
induces a right action of
\Gamma^{\prime}
on
A
with the structure maps
\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}
and

\mu^{\prime}_{i}:A_{i}\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},t}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow A_{i},\quad\mu^{\prime}_{i}(a_{i},\gamma_{1}^{\prime}):=\mu_{i}(a_{i},\beta_{1}(\gamma_{1}^{\prime})).

It can be checked immediately that
\epsilon^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mu^{\prime}_{i}
define a
\Gamma^{\prime}
-action on
A
.

However, this construction is not good enough. If
\beta
is not injective, a
\Gamma^{\prime}
-action on
A
induced from the homomorphism
\beta
does not always exist. In Example

5.5
, we show we can always construct a topological category via pullback, which has a
\Gamma^{\prime}-
action on it.

Example 5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
denote a topological category with a right action of a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
on it, which is given by the structure maps

\epsilon_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad\mu_{i}:A_{i}\times_{\epsilon_{i},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow A_{i}.

Given a groupoid homomorphism

\beta:\Gamma^{\prime}\rightarrow\Gamma,

we can formulate a topological category

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}

with
objects

\{(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{0}\times\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\mid\epsilon_{0}(v_{0})=\beta_{0}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\}

and
the space of morphism

Mor((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),(v^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})):=\{(f:v_{0}\rightarrow v^{\prime}_{0},\hfill 1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}:\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\mid f\text{ is a morphism in }\mathcal{V}.\}\cong Mor(v_{0},v^{\prime}_{0})

and

Mor((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),(v^{\prime}_{0},\eta^{\prime}_{0}))

is empty if
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}
and
\eta^{\prime}_{0}
are different elements.
Note that if
\mathcal{V}
is a
k-
linear category, so is
\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}
.

There is a right
\Gamma^{\prime}
-action on the category

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}

with the structure maps defined by

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}_{0}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{0}\rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\quad

\displaystyle(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\mapsto\gamma^{\prime}_{0}

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{1}\rightarrow\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\quad

\displaystyle(f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}})\mapsto\gamma^{\prime}_{0}

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}_{0}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{0}\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},t}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{0},\quad

\displaystyle((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),\gamma^{\prime}_{1})\mapsto(\mu_{0}(v_{0},\beta(\gamma^{\prime}_{1})),s(\gamma^{\prime}_{1}))

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}_{1}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{1}\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},t}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{1},\quad

\displaystyle((f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}),\gamma^{\prime}_{1})\mapsto(\mu_{1}(f,\beta(\gamma^{\prime}_{1})),1_{s(\gamma^{\prime}_{1})})

for
i=1,2
. These maps satisfy (
5.76
)(
5.77
)(
5.78
)(
5.79
).

In addition, there is a right
\Gamma
-action on the category

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}

with the structure maps defined by

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\times}_{0}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{0}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad

\displaystyle(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\mapsto\beta_{0}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\times}_{1}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{1}\rightarrow\Gamma_{0},\quad

\displaystyle(f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}})\mapsto\beta_{0}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})

\displaystyle\mu^{\times}_{0}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{0}\times_{\epsilon^{\times}_{0},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{0},\quad

\displaystyle((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),\gamma_{1})\mapsto(\mu_{0}(v_{0},\gamma_{1}),s(\gamma_{1}))

\displaystyle\mu^{\times}_{1}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{1}\times_{\epsilon^{\times}_{1},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma_{1}\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})_{1},\quad

\displaystyle((f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}),\gamma_{1})\mapsto(\mu_{1}(f,\gamma_{1}),1_{s(\gamma_{1})})

for
i=1,2
. These maps satisfy (
5.76
)(
5.77
)(
5.78
)(
5.79
).

For any two objects
(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})
and
(v^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})
in
\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}
, there is a one-to-one correspondence between the morphism spaces

Mor((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),(v^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}))\text{ and }Mor(v_{0},v^{\prime}_{0}).

We can define an functor

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}

by sending an object
(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})
to
v_{0}
and sending a morphism
(f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}):(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\rightarrow(v^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})
to
f:v_{0}\rightarrow v_{0}^{\prime}
. It is fully faithful.

A
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant automorphism

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}

is of the form

\displaystyle(v_{0},\gamma_{0}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mapsto(F_{0}(v_{0}),\gamma_{0}^{\prime})

\displaystyle(f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(F_{1}(f),1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}})

where
F=(F_{0},F_{1}):\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
is an
\im\beta
-equivariant automorphism.

There is a well-defined functor

\iota(\beta):\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{V})\rightarrow\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})

sending a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism

F:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}

to the functor which sends an object
(v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})
to

(F(v_{0}),\gamma^{\prime}_{0})

and sends a morphism

(v_{0}\xrightarrow{f}v^{\prime}_{0},1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}})
to

(F(v_{0})\xrightarrow{F(f)}F(v^{\prime}_{0}),1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}})

which is
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant. The functor
\iota
is fully faithful and is injective on objects.
If
\beta
is surjective,
\iota(\beta)
is an isomorphism.

For the category
\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}
and the functor
\iota(\beta)
, we have the conclusions below.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
denote a topological category with a right action of a Lie groupoid
\Gamma
on it. Let
\Gamma"\xrightarrow{\psi}\Gamma^{\prime}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\Gamma
be groupoid homomorphisms.

1.

As shown in Example
5.5
, there is a
\Gamma"
-action on the categories

(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}

and

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}.

These two categories are
\Gamma"
-isomorphic.

2.

The composition

\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{V})\xrightarrow{\iota(\phi)}\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\beta_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\xrightarrow{\iota(\psi)}\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma"}((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})

and

\iota(\phi\circ\psi):\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{V})\rightarrow\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma"}(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})

are equal up to a
\Gamma"
-isomorphism.

Proof.

1.

The objects of
(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}
are

\{((v_{0},\gamma_{0}^{\prime}),\gamma"_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{0}\times\Gamma^{\prime}_{0}\times\Gamma"_{0}\mid\epsilon_{0}(v_{0})=\phi_{0}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime}),\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=\psi_{0}(\gamma"_{0})\}

which is exactly

\{(v_{0},\gamma"_{0})\in\mathcal{V}_{0}\times\Gamma"_{0}\mid\epsilon_{0}(v_{0})=(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}(\gamma"_{0})\}=(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0}.

The space of morphisms

Mor(((v_{0},\gamma_{0}^{\prime}),\gamma"_{0}),((v^{\prime}_{0},\eta_{0}^{\prime}),\eta"_{0}))

is empty if
\gamma"_{0}
and
\eta"_{0}
are different. In this case

\gamma_{0}^{\prime}
and
\eta_{0}^{\prime}
are also different. The space

Mor(((v_{0},\gamma_{0}^{\prime}),\gamma"_{0}),((v^{\prime}_{0},\gamma_{0}^{\prime}),\gamma"_{0})

is

\{((f:v_{0}\rightarrow v^{\prime}_{0},1_{\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}),1_{\gamma"_{0}})\mid f\mbox{ is a morphism in }\mathcal{V}.\}

It is
the same as

Mor((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),(v^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0})).

In other words, we have an isomorphism

j:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}

sending an object
((v_{0},\gamma_{0}^{\prime}),\gamma"_{0})
to
(v_{0},\gamma"_{0})
and a morphism

((f:v_{0}\rightarrow v^{\prime}_{0},1_{\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}),1_{\gamma"_{0}})
to
(f,1_{\gamma"_{0}})
.

Next we show this isomorphism
j
is
\Gamma"
-equivariant.

Apply the construction in Example
5.5
, the structure maps of the
\Gamma"
-action on

(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}

are

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}"_{0}:((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Gamma"_{0},

\displaystyle((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),\gamma"_{0})

\displaystyle\mapsto\gamma"_{0}

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}"_{1}:((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Gamma"_{0},

\displaystyle((f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}),1_{\gamma"_{0}})

\displaystyle\mapsto\gamma"_{0}

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}"_{0}:((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0}\times_{\epsilon"_{0},\Gamma"_{0},t}\Gamma"_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0},

\displaystyle(((v_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{0}),\gamma"_{0}),\gamma"_{1})

\displaystyle\mapsto((\mu_{0}(v_{0},\phi\circ\psi(\gamma"_{1})),s(\gamma^{\prime}_{1})),s(\gamma"_{1}))

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}"_{1}:((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{1}\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},t}\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{1},

\displaystyle(((f,1_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}),1_{\gamma"_{0}}),\gamma"_{1})

\displaystyle\mapsto((\mu_{1}(f,\phi\circ\psi(\gamma"_{1})),1_{s(\gamma^{\prime}_{1})}),1_{\gamma"_{0}})

for
i=1,2
.

And the
\Gamma"
-action on the category

\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0}

with the structure maps defined by

\displaystyle\epsilon"_{0}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Gamma"_{0},

\displaystyle(v_{0},\gamma"_{0})

\displaystyle\mapsto\gamma"_{0}

\displaystyle\epsilon"_{1}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\Gamma"_{0},

\displaystyle(f,1_{\gamma"_{0}})

\displaystyle\mapsto\gamma"_{0}

\displaystyle\mu"_{0}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0}\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma_{0},t}\Gamma"_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{0},

\displaystyle((v_{0},\gamma"_{0}),\gamma"_{1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mu_{0}(v_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)(\gamma"_{1})),s(\gamma"_{1}))

\displaystyle\mu"_{1}:(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{1}\times_{\epsilon"_{1},\Gamma"_{0},t}\Gamma"_{1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{1},

\displaystyle((f,1_{\gamma"_{0}}),\gamma"_{1})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mu_{1}(f,(\phi\circ\psi)(\gamma"_{1})),1_{s(\gamma"_{1})})

for
i=1,2
.

We have

\epsilon^{\prime}"_{i}\circ j=\epsilon"_{i},\quad j(a_{i}\gamma"_{1})=j(a_{i})\gamma"_{1}

for
i=1,2
and any
a_{i}\in((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})_{i}
.

Thus,
j
is
\Gamma"
-equivariant.

2.

From the
\Gamma"
-isomorphism
j
, we can define an isomorphism

j_{*}:\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma"}((\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},\phi_{0}}\Gamma^{\prime}_{0})\times_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{0},\Gamma^{\prime}_{0},\psi_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})\rightarrow\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma"}(\mathcal{V}\times_{\epsilon_{0},\Gamma_{0},(\phi\circ\psi)_{0}}\Gamma"_{0})

by sending an automorphism
F
to
j\circ F\circ j^{-1}
.

It’s straightforwards to check the that

\iota(\phi\circ\psi)=j_{*}\circ\iota(\phi)\circ\iota(\psi).

∎

5.2
The
(3,1)
-presheaf of equivariant 2-vector bundle

We start this section with a motivating example.

Example 5.7
.

Let
G
denote a compact Lie group. Let
X
denote a
G
-space and
V
a
G
-representation. Then a trivial
G
-equivariant vector bundle over
X

is the product
X\times V
with the projection
X\times V\rightarrow X
.

Then we consider a trivial equivariant 2-vector bundle as below. Let
\mathbb{G}
denote a Lie groupoid. Let
\Gamma
denote a Lie groupoid with a right action
\{\epsilon_{i},\mu_{i}\}_{i=1,2}
by
\mathbb{G}
. Let
\mathcal{V}
be a k-linear category with a right action
\{\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\mu^{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\}_{i=1,2}
of
\mathbb{G}
on it.
We consider the category

\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}
with the projection
\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\Gamma
which is a
\mathbb{G}
-equivariant morphism.
The fiber
F_{\gamma_{0}}
at the object
\gamma_{0}
of
\Gamma_{0}
is the full subcategory
(\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{1})^{-1}(\epsilon_{0}(\gamma_{0}))
of
\mathcal{V}
.

Note if
\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}(v_{0})
and
\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}(v^{\prime}_{0})
are different, they don’t belong to the same fibre and there is no
\gamma_{1}
in
\Gamma_{1}
such that
v_{0}=v_{0}^{\prime}\gamma_{1}
.
Let’s consider a decomposition of the groupoid
\Gamma
,

\Gamma=\coprod_{\alpha}\Gamma_{\alpha}

where in each full subgroupoid
\Gamma_{\alpha}
of
\Gamma
, the objects of it are all mapped to the same object under
\epsilon_{0}
. And in different subgroupoid
\Gamma_{\alpha}
and
\Gamma_{\alpha^{\prime}}
, their objects are mapped to different objects under
\epsilon_{0}
.
Thus, for any objects
x
,
y
in
\Gamma_{\alpha}
, the fibres
F_{x}
and
F_{y}
are the same full subcategory of
\mathcal{V}
and we use
F_{\alpha}
to denote it.

Then
\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}
can be viewed as the disjoint union

\coprod_{\alpha}\Gamma_{\alpha}\times F_{\alpha}.

If we pick
\mathbb{G}
to be the groupoid
BG
,
\Gamma
a
G
-space and
\mathcal{V}
a k-linear category with a single object, the category
\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}
is exactly
\Gamma\times\mathcal{V}
. Thus, the above construction is a generalization of the trivial
G
-equivariant vector bundle.

Then we define a
(3,1)
-presheaf
2\textsf{eqVectBd}:\textsf{Gpd}^{op}\to Bicat
, which is a
2-
prestack and then apply the plus construction to
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
in Section
5.4
and get a
(3,1)
-sheaf.

Example 5.8
.

The
(3,1)
-presheaf
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
of equivariant 2-vector bundles:
\textsf{Gpd}^{op}\to 2\textsf{Gpd}
is constructed in the following way.
Let
\mathbb{G}
be a Lie groupoid.

On the level of object, the 2-groupoid
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma)
for
\Gamma
a Lie groupoid with a right action
\{\epsilon_{i},\mu_{i}\}_{i=1,2}
by
\mathbb{G}
, consists of the following data

•

\displaystyle 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma)_{0}=\{\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}\mid

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\;\text{is an object in $2\textsf{Vect}$ with a right action}

\displaystyle\{\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\mu^{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\}_{i=1,2}\text{ of $\mathbb{G}$ on it}\};

•

2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma)_{1}
: a 1-simplex

\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}\xleftarrow{\gamma_{01}}\Gamma\times_{\epsilon_{0},\mathbb{G}_{0},\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}}\mathcal{V}

is a
\mathbb{G}
-bundle isomorphism covering the identity map on
\Gamma
.
For each object
m
in
\Gamma
,
(\gamma_{01})_{m}
gives an element in
\textsf{Auto}_{\mathbb{G}}(F_{m})
. We still use the symbol
(\gamma_{01})_{m}
to denote this element.

For any morphism
g:m_{1}\rightarrow m_{2}
in
\Gamma
, any object
v_{0}
in
\mathcal{V}
with
\epsilon^{\mathcal{V}}_{0}(v_{0})=\epsilon_{0}(m_{2})
, the fibers
F_{m_{1}}
and
F_{m_{2}}
are the same category.

\gamma_{01}((m_{1},v_{0})g)=\gamma_{01}(m_{1},v_{0})g

Thus,

(m_{2},(\gamma_{01})_{m_{2}}(v_{0}g))=(m_{2},(\gamma_{01})_{m_{1}}(v_{0})g).

Thus,

(\gamma_{01})_{m_{2}}(v_{0})=(\gamma_{01})_{m_{1}}(v_{0}g)g^{-1}.

(5.82)

Thus, a 1-simplex
\gamma_{01}
is a functor-valued map

\Gamma_{0}\rightarrow\textsf{Auto}_{\mathbb{G}}(\mathcal{V})

which satisfies (
5.82
).

•

2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma)_{2}
: a 2-simplex with edges
\gamma_{ij}
for
0\leq i<j\leq 2
, or equivalently (in the model of bicategory), a 2-morphism
between
\gamma_{01}\circ\gamma_{12}
and
\gamma_{02}
, is given by a natural transformation valued map
\phi:\Gamma_{0}\to(\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{V}))_{1}
,

Given a morphism
\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi}\Gamma
, the associated functor
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma)\to 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma^{\prime})
is given by pre-composition.
Note that fiber
F_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}
with
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}
an object in
\Gamma
and
F_{\phi(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})}
are the same subcategory of
\mathcal{V}
.

Remark 5.9
.

The
(3,1)
-presheaf
2\textsf{VectBd}
given in Section
4.1
is a special case of the
(3,1)
-presheaf
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
of equivariant 2-vector bundle.

Each smooth manifold can be viewed as a Lie groupoid with only the identity morphisms. If we take the groupoid
\mathbb{G}
in Example
5.8
to be the trivial groupoid and the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
to be a smooth manifold
U
,
2\textsf{VectBd}(U)
is the same as
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(U)
.

In addition, the 2-vector bundle associated to the principal
\mathcal{G}
-bundle given in Section
4.2
is a special case of the equivariant 2-vector bundles, if we take the Lie groupoid
\Gamma
to be a smooth manifold and the groupoid
\mathbb{G}
to be the trivial groupoid and restrict to the case when the
k
-linear category
\mathcal{V}
is
\textsf{Rep}^{\mathcal{G}}
.

The presheaf
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
is in fact a 2-prestack. We first give the definition below.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
\mathfrak{Y}
be a presheaf in bicategories on
(n\textsf{Grpd},\tau)
.

1.

A presheaf
\mathfrak{Y}
is called a
\tau-
prestack, if, for every hypercover
\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
in
(n\textsf{Grpd},\tau)
,
the functor of bicategories

\tau_{\Gamma}:\mathfrak{Y}(X_{\bullet})\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}(\Gamma_{\bullet})

is fully faithful,
i.e. all the functors on Hom categories are equivalences of categories.

2.

A presheaf
\mathfrak{Y}
is called a
\tau-
stack, if, for every hypercover
\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
in
(n\textsf{Grpd},\tau)
, the functor
\tau_{U}
of bicategories is an equivalence of bicategories.

The definition of fully faithful bifunctors in the definition is given in
[
JY21
, Definition 7.0.1]
,
[
GR04
, Definition 2.4.9(ii)]
.

Remark 5.11
.

For any
1-
morphism
f
and
f^{\prime}
in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma)
, let
\alpha:f\Rightarrow f^{\prime}
denote a natural transformation between them, which consists of a natural transformation valued map
\Gamma_{0}\to\textsf{Auto}_{\mathbb{G}}(\mathcal{V})_{1}
, i.e. for each
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\in\Gamma_{0}
, we have an element

\alpha_{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}:f(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})\Rightarrow f^{\prime}(\gamma^{\prime}_{0})

in
(\textsf{Auto}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{V}))_{1}
. The pullbacks of the natural transformation
\alpha
is
\id_{\psi}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\alpha:\psi^{*}f\Rightarrow\psi^{*}f^{\prime}
.

By definition, a hypercover on level 0 is surjective. Thus,
if
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}:f\Rightarrow f^{\prime}
are two different 2-morphisms,
\id_{\psi}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\alpha
and
\id_{\psi}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\alpha^{\prime}
are different. So
\psi^{*}
on the Hom categories is faithful. In addition,
each natural transformation
f\circ\psi\Rightarrow f^{\prime}\circ\psi
can be expressed in the form
\id_{\psi}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\alpha^{\prime}
. Thus,
\psi^{*}
is fully.

Thus, by Definition
5.10
, the
(3,1)
-presheaf
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
is a
2-
prestack.

5.3
The plus construction for
(3,1)
-presheaves over Lie groupoids

We show in this section how to obtain a
2-
stack
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}
on the category
Gpd
of Lie groupoids starting from a
2
-prestack
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}
on
Gpd
.
This is the procedure of higher sheafification.
The main result in this section is a higher version of the plus construction in

[
NS11
, Theorem 3.3]

which obtains a 2-stack on the category of differentiable manifolds starting from a 2-prestack on the category. As indicated in
[
NS11
]
, the plus construction can be generalized to 2-prestacks on the category
Gpd
of Lie groupoids. We show explicitly that this is true in this section.

In the construction of the descent category for the plus construction, instead of the Čech cover, we will use hypercover. Hypercovers were first introduced in
[
AGV72
]
and have been fully studied and used afterwards throughout the homotopy theory of simplicial sheaves,
[
Bro73
]
, etc. Hypercovers for Lie
n-
groupoids play an important role in
[
Wol13
]
and
[
Zhu09
]
. In addition, the cyclic fibrations in the Behrend-Getzler CFO structure for
n
-groupoids objects in a descent category are hypercovers.

Let
\tau
be a singleton Grothendieck
pretopology on the category
\mathsf{Mfd}
of differentiable manifolds.

Definition 5.12
.

For any Lie
n-
groupoid
\Gamma_{\bullet}
and
X_{\bullet}
, a morphism
\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
is a hypercover if, for any
0\leq k\leq n-1
, the natural map from
\Gamma_{k}
to the pull-back of the diagram

\Hom(\partial\Delta[k],\Gamma_{\bullet})\rightarrow\Hom(\partial\Delta[k],X_{\bullet})\leftarrow X_{k}

is a
\tau-
cover and it is an isomorphism for
k=n
.
More explicitly,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow X_{0},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow X_{0}\times_{X_{0}}X_{1}\times_{X_{0}}\Gamma_{0}=(\Gamma_{0}\times\Gamma_{0})\times_{X_{0}\times X_{0}}X_{1}=\Hom(\partial\Delta[1],\Gamma_{\bullet})\times_{\Hom(\partial\Delta[1],X_{\bullet})}X_{1},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\Hom(\partial\Delta[2],\Gamma_{\bullet})\times_{\Hom(\partial\Delta[2],X_{\bullet})}X_{2},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n-1}

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow\Hom(\partial\Delta[n-1],\Gamma_{\bullet})\times_{\Hom(\partial\Delta[n-1],X_{\bullet})}X_{n-1}\mbox{ are all }\tau-\mbox{covers},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{n}

\displaystyle\cong\Hom(\partial\Delta[n],\Gamma_{\bullet})\times_{\Hom(\partial\Delta[n],X_{\bullet})}X_{n},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{k}

\displaystyle\cong\Hom(\partial\Delta[k],\Gamma_{\bullet})\times_{\Hom(\partial\Delta[k],X_{\bullet})}X_{k},\quad\mbox{when }k\geqslant n.

A hypercover is always a weak equivalence.

In the rest of the section, we take the family
\tau
to be the surjective submersions.

Definition 5.13

(descent category)
.

Given a hypercover
\Gamma_{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{\bullet}
in
(\textsf{Gpd},\tau)
,
the descent category is defined by

Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(\Gamma_{\bullet}):=\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(\Gamma_{\bullet}).

Then we describe the bicategory
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
for a Lie groupoid
X_{\bullet}
.

Definition 5.14
.

An object in

\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
consists of a hypercover
U_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
and an object
G
in the descent bicategory
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(U_{\bullet})
.

In order to define 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms between objects with possibly different hypercovers

\pi:U_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
and
\pi^{\prime}:U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
,
and compare them there.

A common refinement of the hypercover
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
is a hypercover.

\xi:Z_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
if and only if there exist hypercovers
s:Z_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow U_{\bullet}
and
s^{\prime}:Z_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow U^{\prime}_{\bullet}
such that the diagram below commutes strictly.

\textstyle{U_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Z_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\scriptstyle{s^{\prime}}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X_{\bullet}}

(5.83)

We have the refinement functors
s^{*}
and
s^{\prime*}

\textstyle{Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(U_{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{*}}
\textstyle{Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(Z_{\bullet})}
\textstyle{Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(U^{\prime}_{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\prime*}}

Definition 5.15
.

•

A 1-morphism between objects
\mathcal{A}=(U_{\bullet},G)
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=(U^{\prime}_{\bullet},G^{\prime})
of
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
consists of a common refinement
Z_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
of the hypercovers
U_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}

and
U^{\prime}_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}

and a 1-morphism
f:s^{*}(G)\rightarrow s^{\prime*}(G^{\prime})
in
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(Z_{\bullet}).

•

Let
m=(Z_{\bullet},f)
and
m^{\prime}=(Z^{\prime}_{\bullet},f^{\prime})
denote two 1-morphisms between objects
\mathcal{A}=(U_{\bullet},G)
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=(U^{\prime}_{\bullet},G^{\prime})
. A 2-morphism between
m
and
m^{\prime}
consists of a common refinement
W_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
of the hypercovers
Z_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}

and
Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}

\textstyle{W_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{t^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{\bullet}}

and a
2-
morphism
\beta:m_{W_{\bullet}}\Rightarrow m^{\prime}_{W_{\bullet}}
of the refined morphisms in
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(W_{\bullet})
.

\textstyle{Z^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{s}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{s}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{U_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{W_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{U^{\prime}_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{Z_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{s^{\prime}}

(5.84)

The diagram (
5.84
) commutes, i.e.
s\circ t=\tilde{s}\circ t^{\prime}
and
s^{\prime}\circ t=\tilde{s}^{\prime}\circ t^{\prime}.

Here
m_{W_{\bullet}}
is a 1-morphism between
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
but with the common refinement
W_{\bullet}
, and 1-morphism

t^{*}f:(s\circ t)^{*}(G)\rightarrow(s^{\prime}\circ t)^{*}(G^{\prime})

in
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(W_{\bullet})
.
Similarly,
m^{\prime}_{W_{\bullet}}
is a 1-morphism between
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
with the common refinement
W_{\bullet}
and 1-morphism

t^{\prime*}f^{\prime}:(\tilde{s}\circ t^{\prime})^{*}(G)\rightarrow(\tilde{s}^{\prime}\circ t^{\prime})^{*}(G^{\prime}),\quad\mbox{ i.e. }t^{\prime*}f^{\prime}:(s\circ t)^{*}(G)\rightarrow(s^{\prime}\circ t)^{*}(G^{\prime})

in
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(W_{\bullet})
.

Thus,
\beta
is a 2-morphism

\beta:t^{*}f\Rightarrow t^{\prime*}f^{\prime}.

•

In addition, two such 2-morphisms
(W_{\bullet},\beta)
and
(W^{\prime}_{\bullet},\beta^{\prime})
must be identified if and only if there exists a further common refinement
V_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
of
W_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
and
W^{\prime}_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}

such that the refined 2-morphisms agree on
V_{\bullet}
.

We have the commutative diagram below.

\textstyle{W_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{t^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{\bullet}}
\textstyle{Z_{\bullet}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\omega^{\prime}}
\textstyle{W^{\prime}_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{t}}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{t}^{\prime}}

\alpha=\omega\circ p=\omega^{\prime}\circ p^{\prime}
.

The refined 2-morphism of
(W_{\bullet},\beta)
is
(V_{\bullet},\omega^{*}\beta)
and the refined 2-morphism of
(W_{\bullet}^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
is
(V_{\bullet},\omega^{\prime*}\beta^{\prime})
.

We have given the definition of objects, 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms in
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
above. Next we define the compositions and identities and check they are well-defined. Let
\mathcal{A}=(U_{\bullet},G)
,
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=(U^{\prime}_{\bullet},G^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}=(U^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet},G^{\prime\prime})

be objects and
m=(Z,f):\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
and
m^{\prime}=(Z^{\prime},f^{\prime}):\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}
be morphisms.

Let
Z^{\prime\prime}:=Z\times_{U^{\prime}_{\bullet}}Z^{\prime}
denote the pullback of the diagram below.

\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{\bullet}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

(5.85)

This pullback exists in
Gpd
and is a common refinement of
U_{\bullet}
and
U_{\bullet}^{\prime}
. The composition
m^{\prime}\circ m
is defined to be the tuple

(Z^{\prime\prime},f^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}\circ f_{Z^{\prime\prime}})

where
f^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}\circ f_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
is the composition of the refined morphism.

By
[
Zhu09
, Lemma 2.7]
,
Z^{\prime\prime}
is a Lie groupoid. And it is a common refinement of
U_{\bullet}
and
U^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet}
. The composition
m^{\prime}\circ m:=(Z^{\prime\prime},f^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}\circ f_{Z^{\prime\prime}})
.
This indeed defines the structure of a bicategory
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X).

In addition, a hypercover leads to the equivalence of bicategories, as shown in the lemma below. We need the conclusion in the proof of
Theorem
5.17
.

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
X_{\bullet}\to Y_{\bullet}
be a hypercover of Lie groupoid, then we have the following equivalence of bicategories:

\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(Y_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\sim}\holim(\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\Rightarrow\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X^{[2]}_{\bullet})\dots)

(5.86)

\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\sim}\holim(\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\Rightarrow\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X^{[2]}_{\bullet})\dots)

(5.87)

thus

\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(Y_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\sim}\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})

(5.88)

Proof.

(
5.86
) follows from
[
NS11
, Theorem 7.5]
since hypercover of Lie groupoid especially implies a level-wise cover, which is a
\tau
-covering of simplicial manifolds. (
5.87
) follows from the fact that the diagonal map

(X_{\bullet}\Leftarrow X_{\bullet}\dots)\to(X_{\bullet}\Leftarrow X^{[2]}_{\bullet}\dots)

is a strong equivalence proven in Lemma 8.1 of the same article. Thus

\holim(\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\Rightarrow\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X^{[2]}_{\bullet})\dots)\xrightarrow{\sim}\holim(\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\Rightarrow\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\dots)\xrightarrow{\cong}\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})

∎

Then we are ready for the main theorem in this section, which is a higher version of
[
NS11
, Theorem 3.3]
.

Theorem 5.17
.

If
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}
is a prestack, then
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}
is a stack. Furthermore the canonical embedding

\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}(X_{\bullet})\rightarrow\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})

is fully faithful for each
X_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

For a hypercover
\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
,

Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}}(\Gamma_{\bullet})=\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(\Gamma_{\bullet}).

To show
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}
is a
\tau-
stack, by Definition
5.10
, we need to show, for every hypercover

c:\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet},

the functor

\tau_{\Gamma}:\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})\rightarrow Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}}(\Gamma_{\bullet})

of bicategories is an equivalence of bicategories.

By Definition
5.14
,
the objects of
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(\Gamma_{\bullet})=Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}}(\Gamma_{\bullet})
are pairs consisting of a hypercover
U_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{\bullet}
and an object
G
in the descent bicategory
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(U_{\bullet})
.
And the objects of
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
are pairs consisting of a hypercover
U_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
and an object
G
in the descent bicategory
Desc_{\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}}(U_{\bullet})
.

And by Definition
5.15
, the
1-
morphisms and
2-
morphisms of both bicategories are defined in the same way.

Thus,
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(\Gamma_{\bullet})
is the subbicategory
of objects of
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
which are defined on coverings

U_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}

And by Lemma
5.16
this subbicategory is equivalent to the bicategory
\tilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}(X_{\bullet})
.

∎

5.4
The
2
-stack of equivariant 2-vector bundles

In this section, we apply the plus construction in Section
5.3
to the
2
-prestack
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
defined in
Section
5.2
and get the 2-stack
2\textsf{eqVectBd}^{+}
of equivariant 2-vector bundles, which describes the entire data of equivariant 2-vector bundles, their 1-morphisms and 2-morphisms. To do this, we will first take the homotopy limit

\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{\bullet})

with
\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
a hypercover of
X_{\bullet}
.
Then we add the data of equivariant descent to
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
and get the
2
-stack
2\textsf{eqVectBd}^{+}
.

5.4.1
The homotopy limit
\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{\bullet})

For a hypercover
\Gamma_{\bullet}\twoheadrightarrow X_{\bullet}
, an object in
\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{\bullet})
consists of

•

on
\Gamma_{0}
, an object
\alpha
in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{0})
, i.e. an element in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{0})_{0}
;

•

on
\Gamma_{1}
, a 1-isomorphism

d_{1}^{*}\alpha\xleftarrow{\gamma}d_{0}^{*}\alpha

in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{1})
, i.e. an element in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{1})_{1}
.

•

on
\Gamma_{2}
, a 2-isomorphism

d^{*}_{1}\gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\Leftarrow}\limits^{\phi}}d^{*}_{2}\gamma\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}d^{*}_{0}\gamma

in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{2})
, i.e. an element in
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{2})_{2}.

•

on
\Gamma_{3}
, a pentagon condition for
\phi
’s

\textstyle{(d^{*}_{2}d^{*}_{3}\gamma\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}d^{*}_{3}d^{*}_{0}\gamma)\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}d^{*}_{0}d^{*}_{1}\gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{d_{2}^{*}d_{1}^{*}\gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d^{*}_{3}\phi}

.

0.80788pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

A 2-morphism in

\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{\bullet})

from
(\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\psi})
to
(\beta,\psi)
consists of

•

on
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{0})
, a 2-morphism
\tilde{\beta}\xrightarrow{\xi}\beta
;

•

A coherence condition held on
2\textsf{eqVectBd}(\Gamma_{1})
,

\psi\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\id\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}d^{*}_{0}\xi)=(d_{1}^{*}\xi\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\id)\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\tilde{\psi}.

This defines the structure of a bicategory. All the coherence conditions are still satisfied. And we have the pullback functors

\alpha^{*}:\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(Y_{\bullet})\rightarrow\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(X_{\bullet})

for all morphisms
\alpha:X_{\bullet}\rightarrow Y_{\bullet}
.

5.4.2
The 2-stack of equivariant 2-vector bundles

Then we add the descent data in the homotopy limit to the 2-prestack
2\textsf{eqVectBd}
.
The
(3,1)
-sheaf

2\textsf{eqVectBd}^{+}:\textsf{Gpd}^{op}\to Bicat

consists of

•

2\textsf{eqVectBd}^{+}(X_{\bullet})_{0}
: an object is a pair
(U_{\bullet},\mathcal{E})
, where
U_{\bullet}
is a hypercover of
X_{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{E}
is an element in
\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(U_{\bullet})_{0}
;

•

2\textsf{eqVectBd}^{+}(X_{\bullet})_{1}
: a 1-morphism between
(U_{\bullet},\mathcal{E})
and
(\tilde{U}_{\bullet},\tilde{\mathcal{E}})
is a pair consisting of a common refinement
V_{\bullet}
of
U_{\bullet}
and
\tilde{U}_{\bullet}
and an element
\alpha\in\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(V_{\bullet})_{1}
;

•

2\textsf{eqVectBd}^{+}(X_{\bullet})_{2}
: a 2-morphism between
(V_{\bullet},\alpha)
and
(\tilde{V}_{\bullet},\tilde{\alpha})
consists of a common refinement
W_{\bullet}
of
V_{\bullet}
and
\tilde{V}_{\bullet}
and an element
\beta\in\holim 2\textsf{eqVectBd}(W_{\bullet})_{2}
. Moreover,
(W_{\bullet},\beta)
and
(\tilde{W}_{\bullet},\tilde{\beta})
are identified if
\beta
and
\tilde{\beta}
agree on a further common refinement of
W_{\bullet}
and
\tilde{W}_{\bullet}
.

6
Application to string 2-group

In this section we apply the construction in Section
4
to the model of string 2-group
[
MRW17
]
.

6.1
2-Representations of string 2-group and the associated 2-vector bundles

By the general definition (
2.9
) of 2-representation, the bicategory of 2-representations of string 2-group
S(QG)
is defined to be

2\textsf{Rep}(B(S(QG)),2\textsf{Vect})=\Hom(B(S(QG)),2\textsf{Vect}),

Applying the examples of 2-representations in Section
2.3
to
S(QG)
, whose underlying Lie groupoid is

\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG\Rightarrow QG
, we have the examples below.

The first is the specific case of Lemma
2.7
.

Lemma 6.1
.

The 2-group
S(QG)
represents on 2
Vect
in the following way

•

Object level: the only object
*
in
BS(QG)
, maps to
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}
. Here
S(QG)
in
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}
also denotes the underlying Lie groupoid of
S(QG)
by abusing of notation.

•

1-Morphism level:

QG\to\Fun(\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)},\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}),\quad g\mapsto F_{g},

with

\begin{split}&F_{g}(\rho):x\mapsto\rho(x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g)(=V_{x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g}),\quad(x\xrightarrow{a}y)\mapsto(V_{x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g}\xrightarrow{\rho(a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g})}V_{y\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g})\\
&F_{g}(\rho\xrightarrow{f}\rho^{\prime})=(F_{g}(\rho)\xrightarrow{F_{g}(f)}F_{g}(\rho^{\prime})),\quad\text{$F_{g}(f)$ is a natural transformation with $F_{g}(f)_{x}=f_{x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g}$,}\end{split}

for all
x,y\in QG
,
(x\xrightarrow{a}y)\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
, and
1
-morphism
f
in
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}
, which is in turn a natural transformation
\rho\Rightarrow\rho^{\prime}
.

•

2-Morphism level: given
(g_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}g^{\prime}_{0})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG

(\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG)\to\{\text{natural transformations }\},\quad g_{1}\mapsto\eta_{g_{1}},

\eta_{g_{1}}(\rho)_{x}:F_{g_{0}}(\rho)(x)\to F_{g^{\prime}_{0}}(\rho)(x),\quad v\mapsto\rho(1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1})(v),\quad\forall v\in F_{g_{0}}(\rho)(x),

(6.90)

where
1_{x}
denotes the identity arrow in
\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
at
x\in QG
.

As shown in Corollary
2.8
, we can also define
\textsf{Rep}_{tr}^{S(QG)}
and have the example below.

Corollary 6.2
.

We have the inclusion from
\textsf{Rep}_{tr}^{S(QG)}
to
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG))}
.
\textsf{Rep}_{tr}^{S(QG)}
is a subcategory of
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}
with objects of the form
S(QG)\longrightarrow\ast/\!\!/\widehat{LG}\longrightarrow\textsf{Rep}_{tr}^{S(QG)}
. There is a 2-representation of
{S(QG)}
with

•

Object level: the only object
*
in the 2-category corresponding to a 2-group, maps to
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}_{tr}
.

•

1-Morphism level:

F:QG\to\Fun(\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}_{tr},\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}_{tr}),\quad g\mapsto F_{g},

where

\begin{split}F_{g}(\rho):x\mapsto V,\quad(x\xrightarrow{a}y)\mapsto(V\xrightarrow{\rho(a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g})}V)\\
F_{g}(\rho\xrightarrow{f}\rho^{\prime})=F_{g}(\rho)\xrightarrow{F_{g}(f)=f}F_{g}(\rho^{\prime})\end{split}

where
\rho\in\textsf{Rep}_{tr}^{S(QG)}
comes with the trivial bundle
V\times QG
, and
f
is a 1-morphism in
\textsf{Rep}^{S(QG)}_{tr}
.

•

2-Morphism level: given
(g_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}g^{\prime}_{0})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG

(\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG)\to\{\text{natural transformations }\},\quad g_{1}\mapsto\eta_{g_{1}},

\eta_{g_{1}}(\rho)_{x}:F_{g_{0}}(\rho)(x)\to F_{g^{\prime}_{0}}(\rho)(x),\quad v\mapsto\rho(1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1})(v),\quad\forall v\in F_{g_{0}}(\rho)(x).

(6.91)

In addition, by Corollary
2.8
, we have the example below involving the positive energy representations of
\textsf{Rep}^{+}(\widehat{LG})
.

Corollary 6.3
.

Using the Lie groupoid presentation
S(QG)=(\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG\Rightarrow QG)
of
\textup{String}(G)
, the corresponding 2-representation is given by

•

The single object
*
is sent to the
k
-linear category
\textsf{Rep}^{+}(\widehat{LG})

•

1-Morphism level:

QG\to\Fun(\textsf{Rep}^{+}(\widehat{LG}),\textsf{Rep}^{+}(\widehat{LG})),\quad g\mapsto F_{g},

where

\begin{split}F_{g}(\rho):x\mapsto(V_{x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g},\rho(x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g)),\quad(x\xrightarrow{a}y)\mapsto(V_{x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g}\xrightarrow{\rho(a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g})}V_{y\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g})\\
F_{g}(\rho\xrightarrow{f}\rho^{\prime})=(F_{g}(\rho)\xrightarrow{F_{g}(f)}F_{g}(\rho^{\prime}))\end{split}

where
f
is a
1
-morphism in
\textsf{Rep}^{+}(\widehat{LG})
, that is a natural transformation
\rho\Rightarrow\rho^{\prime}
with
f_{x}
a positive energy representation, and
F_{g}(f)
is a natural transformation, with
F_{g}(f)_{x}=f_{x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g}
. Note that
F_{g}(f)_{x}
is still a positive energy representation.

•

2-Morphism level: given
(g_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}g^{\prime}_{0})\in\widehat{LG}

\widehat{LG}\to\{\text{natural transformations }\},\quad g_{1}\mapsto\eta_{g_{1}},

\eta_{g_{1}}(\rho)_{x}:F_{g_{0}}(\rho)(x)\to F_{g^{\prime}_{0}}(\rho)(x),\quad v\mapsto\rho(1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1})(v).

(6.92)

Then, apply the construction of 2-vector bundles in Section
4.1
to the case
\mathcal{G}=S(QG)
, we can get the associated 2-vector bundles and principal 2-bundles.

In addition,
As in Section
4.2
, taking
\mathcal{G}=S(QG)
, we can define the
(3,1)
-presheaf
B(S(QG))
over smooth manifolds. After applying the plus construction, we obtain the 2-stack
B(S(QG))^{+}
of principal
S(QG)
-bundles. And we have the associating 2-functor from
B(S(QG))^{+}
to the 2-stack
2\textsf{VectBd}^{+}
of 2-vector bundles.

6.2
The Adjoint
G-
action on 2-representations of string 2-group

Murray, Roberts and Wockel conjectured in
[
MRW17
, Section 4]
that the inclusion from the category of positive energy representations
of
\widehat{LG}
to the category of Hilbert vector bundles
on
S(QG)
preserves the fusion product of positive energy representations.
And they conjectured at the end of
[
MRW17
]
that
the category of positive energy representations
of
\widehat{LG}
could be the ”fixed points” under the adjoint action of
G
onto the category of Hilbert vector bundles
on
S(QG)
.

In this section we show there is a well-defined adjoint action of
G
on 2
\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
. Then we show that, When
\mathcal{G}=S(QG)
and
H=\widehat{LG}
,
by the inclusion

i:\textsf{Rep}H\longrightarrow 2\textsf{Rep}\mathcal{G}

defined in Theorem
2.10
,
\textsf{Rep}\widehat{LG}
is mapped to the fixed points of 2
\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
by the adjoint action of
G
.

First, with the formula (
3.55
) of
\xi
and the formula for the horizontal product
[
MRW17
, Section 3]
in
S(QG)
,
we have the properties of the horizontal product. Here
\underline{e}
is the constant loop in
QG
,

Lemma 6.4
.

For
g\in QG
,

1.

\xi(\underline{e})=\underline{e}
.

2.

\iota\circ\xi(\underline{e})=\underline{e}
.

3.

g\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\underline{e}=\iota\circ\xi(g).

4.

\underline{e}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g=\iota\circ\xi(g).

5.

\underline{e}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\underline{e}=\underline{e}
.

Proof.

1.

By (
3.55
),
\xi(\underline{e})=\underline{e}(0)^{-1}\underline{e}^{\varphi}=e\cdot\underline{e}=\underline{e}
.

2.

By the conclusion above,
\iota\circ\xi(\underline{e})=\iota(\underline{e})=\underline{e}
.

3.

By the formula for the horizontal product
[
MRW17
, Section 3]

and the conclusion above,
g\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\underline{e}=\iota(\xi(g)\xi(\underline{e}))=\iota(\xi(g)\underline{e})=\iota\circ\xi(g).

4.

Similarly,
\underline{e}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g=\iota(\xi(\underline{e})\xi(g))=\iota(\underline{e}\xi(g))=\iota\circ\xi(g).

5.

By the above conclusions,
\underline{e}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\underline{e}=\iota\circ\xi(\underline{e})=\underline{e}
.

∎

First we discuss the adjoint action of
G\subset LG
on
\textsf{Rep}^{+}(\widehat{LG})
.
G
acts by conjugation on both
QG
and
LG
. Let
\alpha\in G
and
g_{0},g_{0}^{\prime}\in QG
. Let

(g_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{1}=(g_{0},h)}g^{\prime}_{0})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG

where
g_{0}^{\prime}=h\cdot g_{0}
.
We can define an ajoint action of
G
on
LG
by

(\alpha\cdot\overline{h})(t):=\alpha\overline{h}(t)\alpha^{-1}

for any
\alpha\in G
and any
\overline{h}\in LG
. To define the adjoint action of
G
on
\widehat{LG}
, consider the central extension

U(1)\rightarrow\widehat{LG}\rightarrow LG.

For
\alpha\in G
, it defines a constant loop in
LG
by
\alpha(t)=\alpha
. This loop
\alpha
can lift to a loop
\widetilde{\alpha}\in\widehat{LG}
.
The adjoint action

(\alpha\cdot h)(t):=\widetilde{\alpha}h(t)\widetilde{\alpha}^{-1}

clearly does not depend on the lift since
U(1)
is the center of
\widehat{LG}
. When there is no confusion, we will still use
\alpha
to
denote this lift
\widetilde{\alpha}
in
\widehat{LG}
.

For any
g_{0}\in QG
and
h\in\widehat{LG}
, the conjugation is defined by

\displaystyle(\alpha\cdot g_{0})(t)

\displaystyle:=\alpha g_{0}(t)\alpha^{-1}

(6.93)

\displaystyle(\alpha\cdot h)(t)

\displaystyle:=\alpha h(t)\alpha^{-1}

(6.94)

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot g_{1}

\displaystyle:=(\alpha\cdot g_{0},\alpha\cdot h)

(6.95)

We have the 1-morphism

(\alpha\cdot g_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot g_{1}}\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG.

The Lie 2-group identity

u=(u_{0},u_{1}):\ast\rightarrow S(QG)

is defined by

\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{1}}
\textstyle{\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{0}}
\textstyle{QG}

where
u_{0}(\ast)=\underline{e}
and
u_{1}(\ast)=1_{\underline{e}}.

For the identity morphism
1_{g_{0}}=(g_{0},\underline{e})
at each object
g_{0}
, we have

\alpha\cdot 1_{g_{0}}=1_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}.

(6.96)

Lemma 6.5
.

For any object
g_{0,1},g_{0,2}\in QG
of
S(QG)
, we have

(\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0,2})=\alpha\cdot(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0,2}).

Proof.

Note that by the formula (
3.55
) of
\xi
, we have

\displaystyle\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})

\displaystyle=\xi(\alpha g_{0}\alpha^{-1})=\alpha g_{0}(0)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}(\alpha g_{0}\alpha^{-1})^{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\alpha g_{0}(0)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}(\alpha g_{0}^{\varphi}\alpha^{-1})=\alpha g_{0}(0)^{-1}g_{0}^{\varphi}\alpha^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha\xi(g_{0})\alpha^{-1}=\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0}).

In addition,

\displaystyle(\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0,1}))(\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0,2}))=(\alpha\xi(g_{0,1})\alpha^{-1})(\alpha\xi(g_{0,2})\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha(\xi(g_{0,1})\xi(g_{0,2}))\alpha^{-1}=\alpha\cdot(\xi(g_{0,1})\xi(g_{0,2}))

Thus,

\displaystyle(\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0,2})=\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0,2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota(\alpha\cdot(\xi(g_{0,1})\xi(g_{0,2})))=\alpha\cdot(\iota(\xi(g_{0,1})\xi(g_{0,2})))=\alpha\cdot(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0,2}).

∎

Recall the formulas (
3.58
)
\xi:\widehat{LG}\to\widehat{\Omega_{\flat}G}

\xi(h)=(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\mathsf{t}(h)))^{-1}\cdot h\cdot\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\mathsf{s}(h))

and (
3.59
)

\sigma:QG\to\widehat{LG}\times_{QG}Q_{\flat,*}G,\quad\gamma\mapsto(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\gamma^{F_{s}}\gamma^{-1}),\gamma(0)^{-1}\gamma^{\varphi}).

Lemma 6.6
.

We have the conclusions about
\xi
and
\sigma
below.

1.

\sigma(\alpha\gamma\alpha^{-1})=\alpha\sigma(\gamma)\alpha^{-1},
for any
\gamma\in QG
;

2.

\xi(1_{g_{0}})=1_{\xi(g_{0})}
, for any
g_{0}\in QG
;

3.

\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{1})=\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{1})
, for any
g_{1}=(g_{0},h)\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
;

4.

1_{g^{\prime}_{0}}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}=(g_{0}^{\prime}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0},\xi(h))
and
g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g^{\prime}_{0}}=(g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g^{\prime}_{0},\xi(h))
, for any
g^{\prime}_{0}\in QG
and any
g_{1}=(g_{0},h)\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
;

Proof.

We prove them one by one.

1.

\displaystyle\sigma(\alpha\gamma\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}((\alpha\gamma\alpha^{-1})^{F_{s}}(\alpha\gamma\alpha^{-1})^{-1}),\;(\alpha\gamma\alpha^{-1})(0)^{-1}\alpha(\gamma^{\varphi})\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\alpha\gamma^{F_{s}}\alpha^{-1}\alpha\gamma^{-1}\alpha^{-1}),\;\alpha\gamma(0)^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\alpha(\gamma^{\varphi})\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\alpha\gamma^{F_{s}}\gamma^{-1}\alpha^{-1}),\;\alpha(\gamma(0)^{-1}\gamma^{\varphi})\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=(\alpha\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\gamma^{F_{s}}\gamma^{-1})\alpha^{-1},\;\alpha(\gamma(0)^{-1}\gamma^{\varphi})\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=\alpha\sigma(\gamma)\alpha^{-1}.

2.

\xi(1_{g_{0}})=(\xi(g_{0}),\xi(\underline{e}))\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
.

\displaystyle\xi(\underline{e})

\displaystyle=(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\mathsf{t}(1_{g_{0}})))^{-1}\cdot\underline{e}\cdot\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\mathsf{s}(1_{g_{0}}))

\displaystyle=(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(g_{0}))^{-1}\cdot\underline{e}\cdot\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(g_{0})

\displaystyle=(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(g_{0}))^{-1}\cdot\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(g_{0})

\displaystyle=\underline{e}

Thus,
\xi(1_{g_{0}})=(\xi(g_{0}),\underline{e})=1_{\xi(g_{0})}
in
\widehat{\Omega_{\flat}G}\ltimes Q_{\flat,*}G
.

3.

\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{1})=(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0}),\xi(\alpha\cdot h))=(\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0}),\xi(\alpha\cdot h))
.

\displaystyle\xi(\alpha\cdot h)

\displaystyle=(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\mathsf{t}(\alpha h\alpha^{-1})))^{-1}\cdot(\alpha h\alpha^{-1})\cdot\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\mathsf{s}(\alpha h\alpha^{-1}))

\displaystyle=(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\alpha g_{0}\alpha^{-1}))^{-1}\cdot(\alpha h\alpha^{-1})\cdot\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(\alpha hg_{0}\alpha^{-1})

\displaystyle=\alpha(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(g_{0}))^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\cdot(\alpha h\alpha^{-1})\cdot\alpha(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(hg_{0}))\alpha^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(g_{0}))^{-1}h(\pr_{\widehat{LG}}\circ\sigma(hg_{0}))\alpha^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha\xi(h)\alpha^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha\cdot\xi(h)

Thus,
\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{1})=(\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0}),\alpha\cdot\xi(h))
.

4.

Let
g_{1}=(g_{0},h)
,
g_{1}^{\prime}=(g_{0}^{\prime},h^{\prime})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG

Claim: The horizontal product

g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime}=(g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0}^{\prime},h\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}h^{\prime})

is given by

g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime}=(\iota(\xi(g_{0})\xi(g_{0}^{\prime})),\iota(\xi(h)\xi(h^{\prime}))).

(6.97)

Proof of the claim.

The target of
g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g^{\prime}_{1}
is

\displaystyle\iota(\xi(h)\xi(h^{\prime}))\cdot\iota(\xi(g_{0})\xi(g_{0}^{\prime}))=\iota(\xi(h)\xi(h^{\prime})\cdot\xi(g_{0})\xi(g_{0}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota((\xi(h)\cdot\xi(g_{0}))(\xi(h^{\prime})\cdot\xi(g^{\prime}_{0})))=\iota(\xi(h\cdot g_{0})\xi(h^{\prime}\cdot g^{\prime}_{0}))

is the horizontal product of the targets of
g_{1}
and
g_{1}^{\prime}
. ∎

Let
1_{g^{\prime}_{0}}=(g^{\prime}_{0},\underline{e})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
. Then
g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g^{\prime}_{0}}=(g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0}^{\prime},h\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\underline{e}).

\displaystyle h\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\underline{e}

\displaystyle=\iota(\xi(h)\xi(\underline{e}))

\displaystyle=\iota(\xi(h)\underline{e})=\iota\xi(h)

\displaystyle=\xi(h).

Similarly, for
1_{g^{\prime}_{0}}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}=(g^{\prime}_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0},\underline{e}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}h),
we have

\displaystyle\underline{e}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}h

\displaystyle=\iota(\xi(\underline{e})\xi(h))

\displaystyle=\iota\xi(h)=\xi(h).

∎

Lemma 6.7
.

The formulas (
6.93
)(
6.94
)(
6.95
)
defines a strict 2-functor

\alpha_{\bullet}:BS(QG)\rightarrow BS(QG)

\textstyle{G_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{G_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\textstyle{G_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ast\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{0}}
\textstyle{\ast}

in the following way.

•

On the object level,
\alpha_{0}
sends the single object
*

to the single object
*
;

•

On the level of 1-morphisms,
\alpha_{1}
sends
g_{0}
to
\alpha\cdot g_{0}
;

•

On the level of 2-morphisms,
\alpha_{2}
sends a
2-
morphism
g_{0}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}g_{0}^{\prime}
to
\alpha\cdot g_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot g_{1}}\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0}
.

Proof.

First we show
\alpha_{\bullet}
satisfies the conditions below.

1.

\alpha_{\bullet}
strictly preserves the Lie 2-group identity
u
.

\alpha_{1}(u_{0})=(\alpha\cdot u_{0})(\ast)=\alpha\cdot u_{0}(\ast)=\alpha\cdot\underline{e}=\underline{e}
.
And by (
6.96
),
\alpha_{2}(u_{1})=u_{1}
.

2.

\alpha_{\bullet}
strictly preserves the horizontal products and vertical products.

By Lemma
6.5
,
\alpha_{1}(g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\alpha_{1}(g_{0,2})=\alpha_{1}(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0,2})

for any
g_{0,1},g_{0,2}\in QG
.

For
g_{0,1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}=(g_{0,1},h)}g_{0,2}
and
g^{\prime}_{0,1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{0,1},h^{\prime})}g^{\prime}_{0,2}
, by
Lemma
6.5
and Lemma
6.6

we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\alpha\cdot(g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime})=\alpha\cdot(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g^{\prime}_{0,1},h\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(\alpha\cdot(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g^{\prime}_{0,1}),\alpha\cdot(h\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}h^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=((\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0,1}),\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(h)\xi(h^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=((\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0,1}),\iota(\alpha(\xi(h)\xi(h^{\prime}))\alpha^{-1}))

\displaystyle=((\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0,1}),\iota((\alpha\xi(h)\alpha^{-1})(\alpha\xi(h^{\prime})\alpha^{-1})))

\displaystyle=((\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0,1}),\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot h)\xi(\alpha\cdot h^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=((\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g^{\prime}_{0,1}),(\alpha\cdot h)\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot h^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{2}(g_{1})\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{\prime})

In addition,

\alpha_{2}
sends the vertical product, i.e. the composition of
2-
morphisms,

g_{0,1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}}g_{0,2}\xrightarrow{g_{1}^{\prime}}g_{0,3}

to

\alpha\cdot g_{0,1}\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot g_{1}}\alpha\cdot g_{0,2}\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot g_{1}^{\prime}}\alpha\cdot g_{0,3}.

We have
\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{v}}}g_{1})=\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{\prime})\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{v}}}\alpha_{2}(g_{1})
.

3.

\alpha_{\bullet}
strictly preserves the horizontal inverses and vertical inverses.

For
g_{0,1},g_{0,2}\in QG
, if

g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0,2}=\underline{e},

then, by Lemma
6.5
,

(\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0,2})=\alpha\cdot(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0,2})=\alpha\cdot\underline{e}=\underline{e}.

Thus,
\alpha_{1}(g_{0,2})=\alpha\cdot g_{0,2}
is the horizontal inverse of
\alpha_{1}(g_{0,1})
.

In addition, for
g_{0,1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}=(g_{0,1},h)}g_{0,2}
and
g^{\prime}_{0,1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}^{\prime}=(g^{\prime}_{0,1},h^{\prime})}g^{\prime}_{0,2}
, by the proof above,
we have

\alpha_{2}(g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime})=\alpha_{2}(g_{1})\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{\prime}).

Thus, if
g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime}=(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g^{\prime}_{0,1},\underline{e})
,

\alpha_{2}(g_{1})\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{\prime})=\alpha_{2}(g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}^{\prime})=(\alpha\cdot(g_{0,1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g^{\prime}_{0,1}),\alpha\cdot\underline{e})=((\alpha\cdot g_{0,1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0,2}),\underline{e}).

Thus,
\alpha_{2}(g_{1})
is the horizontal inverse of
\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{\prime})
.
\alpha_{\bullet}
preserves horizontal inverses.

For
g_{0,1}\xrightarrow{g_{1}=(g_{0,1},h)}g_{0,2}
and
g_{0,2}\xrightarrow{g_{1}^{-1}=(g_{0,2},h^{-1})}g_{0,1}
,
since

\alpha_{2}(g_{1})\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{v}}}\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{-1})=\alpha_{2}(g_{1}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{v}}}g_{1}^{-1})=\alpha_{2}(1_{g_{0,1}})=1_{\alpha\cdot g_{0,1}}

thus,
\alpha_{2}(g_{1}^{-1})=\alpha_{2}(g_{1})^{-1}
.
We applied Lemma
6.6
(2) and Lemma
6.7
(2) in the proof above.

We have shown all the diagrams below commute. Next we will show that the left and right unit constraints
l
and
r
are also compatible with
\alpha_{\bullet}
.
