131.52621pt\raise-88.41663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 190.9053pt\raise-88.41663pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{QG}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

4.

\alpha_{\bullet}
strictly preserve the left unit constraint.

By the definition
[
MRW17
, Section 3, (15)]

of the left unit constraint
l
of
S(QG)
,
l_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}
is the composition

l_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}:u_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0})=\iota(\xi(\iota(\underline{e}))\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0}))\xrightarrow{\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})})}\iota(\underline{e}\cdot\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0}))=\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0}))\xrightarrow{\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}}\alpha\cdot g_{0}

and
\alpha\cdot l_{g_{0}}
is the composition

\alpha\cdot l_{g_{0}}:\alpha\cdot(u_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0})=\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(\iota(\underline{e}))\xi(g_{0}))\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(g_{0})})}\alpha\cdot\iota(\underline{e}\cdot\xi(g_{0}))=\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(g_{0}))\xrightarrow{{\alpha}\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}}\alpha\cdot g_{0}

We want to show they are the same functor.

In other words, for each
g_{0}\in QG
we have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{u_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})})}
\scriptstyle{\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(g_{0})})}
\textstyle{\iota(\underline{e}\cdot\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}}
\textstyle{\alpha\cdot\iota(\underline{e}\cdot\xi(g_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\alpha}\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}}
\textstyle{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}

(6.98)

Note that
\xi(\iota(\underline{e}))=\xi(\underline{e})
and, by Lemma
6.5
,

\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\cdot\id_{\xi(g_{0})})=\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})}).

Next we show
\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}={\alpha}\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}
and
\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})})=\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(g_{0})})
respectively.

In
[
MRW17
, Section 3]
Murray, Roberts and Wockel constructed the counit

\epsilon:\xi\iota\Rightarrow\id

after choosing the quasi-inverse
\xi
of
\iota

and they constructed the unit

\eta:\iota\xi\Rightarrow\id,

which at each
g_{0}\in QG
is the unique lift through
\xi
of

\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g_{0})}:\xi(g_{0})\rightarrow\xi(\iota(\xi(g_{0})))).

(6.99)

By Lemma
6.6
, for each
g_{0}\in Q_{\flat,*}G
and
g_{1}\in\widehat{\Omega_{\flat}G}\ltimes Q_{\flat,*}G
,
we have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}:\xi\iota(\alpha\cdot g_{0})=\alpha\cdot(\xi\iota(g_{0}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\alpha\cdot g_{0}

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot g_{1}}:\xi\iota(\alpha\cdot g_{1})=\alpha\cdot(\xi\iota(g_{1}))

\displaystyle\rightarrow\alpha\cdot g_{1}

We have the formula from (
3.59
) that

\epsilon_{g_{0}}=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\gamma^{F_{s}}\gamma^{-1}))^{-1},

where
g_{0}=\gamma(t)
. And

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}((\alpha\cdot\gamma)^{F_{s}}(\alpha\cdot\gamma)^{-1}))^{-1}=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\alpha\gamma^{F_{s}}\alpha^{-1}\alpha\gamma^{-1}\alpha^{-1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\alpha(\gamma^{F_{s}}\gamma^{-1})\alpha^{-1}))^{-1}=\alpha(\pi_{2}\circ\sigma^{\prime}(\gamma^{F_{s}}\gamma^{-1}))^{-1}\alpha^{-1}

\displaystyle=\alpha\cdot\epsilon_{g_{0}}.

By the property of functors,

\epsilon^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}=\alpha\cdot\epsilon^{-1}_{g_{0}}.

By (
6.99
),
\eta_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}
is the unique lift through
\xi
of

\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})}=\epsilon^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0})}=\alpha\cdot\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g_{0})}.

Thus,

\eta_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}=\alpha\cdot\eta_{g_{0}}

and

\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}=\alpha\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}.

By the definition of
\alpha
on the
2-
morphisms, we have the equalities

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}

\displaystyle={\alpha}\cdot\epsilon_{g_{0}},\quad\epsilon^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}={\alpha}\cdot\epsilon^{-1}_{g_{0}}

(6.100)

\displaystyle\eta_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}

\displaystyle={\alpha}\cdot\eta_{g_{0}},\quad\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}={\alpha}\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}

(6.101)

Thus,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot{\underline{e}}}\id_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}\mbox{ by }\eqref{gactionid1}

\displaystyle=\alpha\cdot(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{g_{0}})

\displaystyle\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})})=\iota(\alpha\cdot(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{g_{0}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\underline{e}}\id_{\xi(g_{0})})

In fact they are both identity since
\xi(\iota(\underline{e}))=\xi(\underline{e})=\underline{e}
.

Thus,
l_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}
and
\alpha\cdot l_{g_{0}}
are the same functor.
The diagram (
6.98
) commute.

5.

Compatibility with the right unit constraint.

By the definition
[
MRW17
, Section 3, (14)]

of the left unit constraint
l
of
S(QG)
,
r_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}

is the composition

r_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}:(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}u_{0}=\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\xi(\iota(\underline{e})))\xrightarrow{\iota(\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})}\epsilon_{\underline{e}})}\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\cdot\underline{e})=\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0}))\xrightarrow{\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}}\alpha\cdot g_{0}

and
\alpha\cdot r_{g_{0}}
is the composition

\alpha\cdot r_{g_{0}}:\alpha\cdot(g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}u_{0})=\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(g_{0})\xi(\iota(\underline{e})))\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot\iota(\id_{\xi(g_{0})}\epsilon_{\underline{e}})}\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(g_{0})\cdot\underline{e})=\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(g_{0}))\xrightarrow{{\alpha}\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}}\alpha\cdot g_{0}

Similarly, the first map of each composition,
\iota(\id_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})}\epsilon_{\underline{e}})
and
\alpha\cdot\iota(\id_{\xi(g_{0})}\epsilon_{\underline{e}})
,
are both the identity map. And by the analysis (
6.101
),
\eta^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot g_{0}}={\alpha}\cdot\eta^{-1}_{g_{0}}
. So the two compositions
give the same map.

6.

Compatibility with the associator.

For any 1-morphisms

*\xrightarrow{f}*\xrightarrow{g}*\xrightarrow{h}*,

By the definition
[
MRW17
, Section 3, (13)]
of the associator
a
of
S(QG)
,

a_{\alpha\cdot f,\alpha\cdot g,\alpha\cdot h}
is the composition

\displaystyle a_{\alpha\cdot f,\alpha\cdot g,\alpha\cdot h}:\iota(\xi(\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\xi(\alpha\cdot g)))\xi(\alpha\cdot h))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\xi(\alpha\cdot g)}\xi(\alpha\cdot h))}\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\xi(\alpha\cdot g)\xi(\alpha\cdot h))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g)\xi(\alpha\cdot h)})}\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\xi(\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g)\xi(\alpha\cdot h))))

We have

\displaystyle\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\xi(\alpha\cdot g)}\xi(\alpha\cdot h))=\iota(\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot(\xi(f)\xi(g))}(\alpha\cdot\xi(h)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota((\alpha\cdot\epsilon_{\xi(f)\xi(g)})(\alpha\cdot\xi(h)))=\iota(\alpha\cdot(\epsilon_{\xi(f)\xi(g)}\xi(h)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(f)\xi(g)}\xi(h))

\displaystyle\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot f)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g)\xi(\alpha\cdot h)})=\iota((\alpha\cdot\xi(f))\epsilon^{-1}_{(\alpha\cdot\xi(g))(\alpha\cdot\xi(h))})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota((\alpha\cdot\xi(f))\epsilon^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot(\xi(g)\xi(h))})=\iota\alpha\cdot(\xi(f)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g)\xi(h)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot(\iota(\xi(f)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g)\xi(h)})).

Thus,
a_{\alpha\cdot f,\alpha\cdot g,\alpha\cdot h}
and

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(a_{f,g,h}):\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(\iota(\xi(f)\xi(g)))\xi(h))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(f)\xi(g)}\xi(h))}\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(f)\xi(g)\xi(h))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(f)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g)\xi(h)})}\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(f)\xi(\iota(\xi(g)\xi(h))))

are the same functor.

Thus,
\alpha_{\bullet}
is a well-defined strict 2-functor.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in G
,
the composition
\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}
and the multiplication
(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})_{\bullet}
are the same 2-functor from
BS(QG)

to itself.

Especially, for each
\alpha\in G
,
\alpha_{\bullet}\circ(\alpha^{-1})_{\bullet}
and
(\alpha^{-1}\circ\alpha)_{\bullet}
are both
identities.
Thus, each
\alpha\in G
gives an equivalence of the bicategories
\alpha_{\bullet}:BS(QG)\rightarrow BS(QG)
.

Proof.

Below we verify that
\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}=(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})_{\bullet}
.

For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in G
, we have the composition

\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}:BS(QG)\rightarrow BS(QG)

defined by the data below.

•

\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}
sends the single object
*
to
*
.

•

\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}
sends each
1-
morphism
g_{0}
in
BS(QG)
to

(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})\cdot g_{0}=\alpha\cdot(\alpha^{\prime}\cdot g_{0}).

•

It sends each
2-
morphism
g_{1}
to

(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})\cdot g_{1}=\alpha\cdot(\alpha^{\prime}\cdot g_{1}).

•

(\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet})
sends the unit
u
to itself since

(\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet})(u)=\alpha_{\bullet}\circ(\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}(u))=\alpha_{\bullet}(u)=u.

Thus, the
2-
functor
\alpha_{1}\circ\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}
.

∎

Moreover, we have the conclusion below.

Lemma 6.9
.

We have a
G-
action on the
BS(QG)
defined by the data below. (The definition of group actions on bicategories is from
[
Hes17
, Section 2.2, Definition 2.14, Remark 2.16]
and
[
BGM19
, Section 2, page 7]
.)

•

For each
\alpha\in G
, we have the strict 2-functor
\alpha_{\bullet}:BS(QG)\rightarrow BS(QG)
defined in Lemma

6.7
, which, is an equivalence of bicategories;

•

For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in G
, the natural equivalences

(\chi_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},\chi_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{0}):\alpha_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{\bullet}\Rightarrow(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})_{\bullet}

are defined to be identity.

•

The natural equivalence

\kappa:\id\Rightarrow e_{\bullet}

is defined to be the identity, where

e_{\bullet}:BS(QG)\rightarrow BS(QG)

is the functor corresponding to the identity element
e\in G
.

•

For any
1-
morphisms
g,h,f
in
S(QG)
, the invertible modifications

\displaystyle\omega_{g,h,f}:\chi_{gh,f}\circ(\chi_{g,h}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{f})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\chi_{g,hf}\circ(1_{g}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\chi_{h,f})

\displaystyle\kappa_{g}:\chi_{\underline{e},g}\circ(\kappa\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow 1_{g}

\displaystyle\xi_{g}:\chi_{g,\underline{e}}\circ(1_{g}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\kappa)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow 1_{g}

are
all defined to be the identity.

Proof.

Since the invertible 2-morphisms and modifications in the definitions are all identity, it’s straightforward to check that They satisfy the coherence conditions, i.e.
[
Hes17
, Section 2.2, HTA1, HTA2]
and
[
BGM19
, Section 2, (2.1)(2.2)]
.

∎

Lemma 6.10
.

For each
\alpha\in G
, the 2-functor

\alpha_{\bullet}:BS(QG)\rightarrow BS(QG)

defines a 2-functor

\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}:2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)\rightarrow 2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)

in the following way.

•

On the object level,
sending an
S(QG)-
represenation

A=(\mathcal{V},F,\eta)

to

\alpha^{*}_{0}(A)=(\mathcal{V},\;\alpha\cdot F,\;\alpha\cdot\eta)

where

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot F_{g_{0}}

\displaystyle:=F_{\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}};

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot\eta_{g_{1}}

\displaystyle:=\eta_{\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1}}.

for any
g_{0}\in QG
and
g_{1}\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
.

•

Let
A_{i}:=(\mathcal{V}_{i},F_{i},\eta_{i})
with
i=1,2,\cdots
. On the level of 1-morphisms, with the definition of 1-morphisms in the bicategory of
2-
representations in Example
2.5
,
sending

f=(T(*),T):A_{1}\longrightarrow A_{2}

to

\alpha^{*}_{1}(f)=(T(*),\;\alpha^{*}_{2}(T)):\alpha^{*}_{0}(A_{1})\longrightarrow\alpha^{*}_{0}(A_{2})

where

\alpha^{*}_{2}(T):T(\ast)^{*}\circ(\alpha\cdot F_{2})\Rightarrow T(\ast)_{*}\circ(\alpha\cdot F_{1})

is given by the composition

\displaystyle T(\ast)^{*}\circ(\alpha\cdot F_{2})=T(\ast)^{*}\circ(F_{2}\circ\alpha)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{a}(T(\ast)^{*}\circ F_{2})\circ\alpha\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\Rightarrow}\limits^{T\circ 1_{\alpha}}}(T(\ast)_{*}\circ F_{1})\circ\alpha

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{a}T(\ast)_{*}\circ(F_{1}\circ\alpha)=T(\ast)_{*}\circ(\alpha\cdot F_{1})

where
a
is the associator and
T\circ 1_{\alpha}
is below.

\textstyle{G_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nearrow_{T}}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\textstyle{\textsf{Auto}(D_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T(\ast)_{*}}
\textstyle{\textsf{Auto}(D_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T(\ast)^{*}}
\textstyle{\Fun(D_{1},D_{2})}

(6.102)

Proof.

We show
\alpha_{\bullet}
satisfies the conditions below.

1.

\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}
strictly preserves the Lie 2-group identity
\underline{0}
in
2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
, which is defined by the data below.

•

On the level of objects, sends
*
to the category consisting of only one object
0
and one morphisms at
0
, the identity morphism.

•

On the level of 1-morphisms, all the elements in
QG
is mapped to the identity morphism at
0
.

•

On the level of 2-morphisms, all the elements in
\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
are sent to the identity natural transformation from the identity morphism to itself.

\alpha^{*}_{0}(\underline{0})=\underline{0}
by definition.

The identity morphism in
2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
from
\underline{0}
to itself

\id=(\id,\id):\underline{0}\longrightarrow\underline{0}

is sent to

\alpha^{*}_{1}(\id)=(\id,\;\alpha^{*}_{2}(\id))=(\id,\id)=\id.

2.

\alpha_{\bullet}
strictly preserves the horizontal products and vertical products.

For
S(QG)-
representations

A_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},F_{i},\eta_{i})

where
i=1,2,3,4
, the
1-
morphisms

f_{1},f_{2}:A_{1}\rightarrow A_{2};\quad f_{3},f_{4}:A_{3}\rightarrow A_{4}

and the
2-
morphisms

\beta_{1}:f_{1}\Rightarrow f_{2}\mbox{ and }\beta_{2}:f_{3}\Rightarrow f_{4},

\alpha^{*}_{2}(\beta_{1}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\beta_{2})=\alpha^{*}_{2}(\beta_{1})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\alpha^{*}_{2}(\beta_{2}).

If we have the composition of
1-
morphisms in 2
\textsf{Rep}S(QG)

A_{1}\xrightarrow{f}A_{2}\xrightarrow{g}A_{3}

\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}
sends it to

\alpha_{0}^{*}(A_{1})\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}^{*}(f)}\alpha^{*}_{0}(A_{2})\xrightarrow{\alpha_{1}^{*}(g)}\alpha^{*}_{0}(A_{3})

3.

\alpha_{\bullet}
strictly preserves the inverses.

For
S(QG)-
representations

A_{i}=(\mathcal{V}_{i},F_{i},\eta_{i})

where
i=1,2
, and the
1-
morphisms

f:A_{1}\rightarrow A_{2};\quad f^{-1}:A_{2}\rightarrow A_{1},

\id=\alpha^{*}_{1}(f\circ f^{-1})=\alpha^{*}_{1}(f)\circ\alpha^{*}_{1}(f^{-1}).

4.

Since
2\textsf{Vect}
is a strict
2-
category, 2
\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
is a strict 2-category as well. So the associator, left and right unit constraints
in it are all identity.

∎

We have the corollary of Lemma
6.8
and Lemma
6.10
below, which can be checked straightforwards.

Lemma 6.11
.

For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in G
,

\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime*}_{\bullet}
and
(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})^{*}_{\bullet}
are the same 2-functor from 2
\textsf{Rep}(S(QG))

to itself.

Especially, for each
\alpha\in G
,
\alpha\alpha^{-1}
and
\alpha^{-1}\alpha
are both
identities.
Thus, each
\alpha\in G
gives an equivalence of the bicategories
\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}:2\textsf{Rep}(S(QG))\rightarrow 2\textsf{Rep}(S(QG))
.

Lemma 6.12
.

The
G-
action on the
BS(QG)
in Lemma
6.9
induces a
G-
action on 2
\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
.

•

For each
\alpha\in G
, we have the strict 2-functor

\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}:2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)\rightarrow 2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)

defined in
Lemma
6.10
.

•

For any
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in G
, the natural equivalences

\chi^{*}:\alpha^{*}_{\bullet}\circ\alpha^{\prime*}_{\bullet}\Rightarrow(\alpha\alpha^{\prime})^{*}_{\bullet}

is defined to be the identity.

•

The natural equivalence

\kappa^{*}:\id\Rightarrow e^{*}_{\bullet}

is defined to be the identity, where
e^{*}_{\bullet}:2\textsf{Rep}(S(QG))\rightarrow 2\textsf{Rep}(S(QG))
is the functor corresponding to the identity element
e\in G
.

•

For any
g,h,f\in G
, the invertible modifications

\displaystyle\omega^{*}_{g,h,f}:\chi^{*}_{gh,f}\circ(\chi^{*}_{g,h}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{f})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\chi^{*}_{g,hf}\circ(1_{g}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\chi^{*}_{h,f})

\displaystyle\kappa^{*}_{g}:\chi^{*}_{\underline{e},g}\circ(\kappa^{*}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow 1_{g}

\displaystyle\xi^{*}_{g}:\chi^{*}_{g,\underline{e}}\circ(1_{g}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}\kappa^{*})

\displaystyle\Rightarrow 1_{g}

are
all defined to be the identity.

Proof.

Since the invertible 2-morphisms and modifications in the definitions are all identity, it’s straightforward to check that They satisfy the coherence conditions, i.e.
[
Hes17
, Section 2.2, HTA1, HTA2]
and
[
BGM19
, Section 2, (2.1)(2.2)]
.

∎

Example 6.13
.

Below is the comparison of the
2-
Representation

A=(C_{V},f_{V},\eta_{V})

in Corollary
2.9
,
A
and

\alpha\cdot A=(C_{V},\alpha\cdot f_{V},\alpha\cdot\eta_{V}).

For
\alpha\cdot f_{V}
, it sends an object
g_{0}
of
QG
to
\alpha\cdot f_{V,g_{0}}
which is defined by

\alpha\cdot f_{V,g_{0}}:C_{V}\longrightarrow C_{V},\quad\alpha\cdot f_{V,g_{0}}(\rho):x\mapsto\rho(x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}))=V_{0},

(x\xrightarrow{a}y)\mapsto(V_{0}\xrightarrow{\rho(a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}})}V_{0})

Let
a=(x,h)
. By the property of horizontal product in Lemma
6.6
,

\displaystyle a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}}

\displaystyle=(x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}),\;\xi(h))

\displaystyle a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g_{0}}

\displaystyle=(x\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{0},\;\xi(h)).

Thus,

\rho(a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}})=\rho(a\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}1_{g_{0}}).

And

\alpha\cdot f_{V,g_{0}}(\rho\xrightarrow{b}\rho^{\prime})=(f_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}}(\rho)\xrightarrow{f_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}}(b)}f_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}}(\rho^{\prime})),\quad f_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}}(b)=b

for any
\rho\in C_{V}
,
x\in QG
,
(x\xrightarrow{a}y)\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
, and
(\rho\xrightarrow{b}\rho^{\prime})
a morphism in
C_{V}
.

Thus,

\alpha\cdot f_{V}=f_{V}.

(6.103)

And, given
g_{1}=(g_{0}\xrightarrow{h}g^{\prime}_{0})\in\widehat{LG}\ltimes QG
,
\alpha\cdot\eta_{V}
is defined by

\eta_{V}:G_{1}\to\{\text{natural transformations }\},\quad g_{1}\mapsto\eta_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1}},

\eta_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1}}:f_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}}\to f_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g^{\prime}_{0}},

with

\eta_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1}}(\rho)_{x}=\rho(1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1})),

component-wise. By (
6.103
), both
\eta_{V,g_{1}}(\rho)_{x}
and
\eta_{V,\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1}}(\rho)_{x}

are functors from
f_{V,g_{0}}(\rho)_{x}
to
f_{V,g^{\prime}_{0}}(\rho)_{x}
, i.e. from
V_{0}
to
V_{0}
.
By the property of horizontal product in Lemma
6.6
,

\displaystyle 1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1}

\displaystyle=(x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0},\;\xi(h))

(6.104)

\displaystyle 1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1})

\displaystyle=(x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}),\;\xi(\alpha^{-1}\cdot h))=(x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{0}),\;\alpha^{-1}\cdot\xi(h)).

(6.105)

Thus,
\rho(1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha^{-1}\cdot g_{1}))
may not always equal to
\rho(1_{x}\mathbin{\cdot_{\textup{h}}}g_{1})
for any
\rho\in C_{V}
.
So

\alpha\cdot\eta_{V}
may not be the same natural transformation as
\eta_{V}
.

Then we show that, for any
\alpha\in QG
, for
A=(C_{V},f_{V},\eta_{V},\phi,\psi)
in
2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
, there is a
1-
equivalence in
S(QG)

T_{\alpha}:A\rightarrow\alpha\cdot A

where

\alpha\cdot A=(C_{V},\alpha\cdot f_{V},\alpha\cdot\eta_{V},\alpha\cdot\phi,\alpha\cdot\psi).

As we show above,
\alpha\cdot f_{V}=f_{V}
.
And, as for the natural transformations
(\psi,\phi)

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern (6.106)

where
(F,\eta)
here is
(f_{V},\eta_{V})
or
(\alpha\cdot f_{V},\alpha\cdot\eta_{V})
.

By the formula (
2.29
), the functor

\alpha\cdot\psi_{(g_{0},\hat{g}_{0})}:(\alpha\cdot f)_{V,g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\hat{g}_{0}}\rightarrow(\alpha\cdot f)_{V,g_{0}}\circ(\alpha\cdot f)_{V,\hat{g}_{0}}

is defined by

\alpha\cdot\psi_{(g_{0},\hat{g}_{0})}(\rho)(x):(\alpha\cdot f)_{V,g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\hat{g}_{0}}(\rho)(x)\rightarrow(\alpha\cdot f)_{V,g_{0}}\circ(\alpha\cdot f)_{V,\hat{g}_{0}}(\rho)(x),\quad\alpha\cdot\psi_{(g_{0},\hat{g}_{0})}(\rho)(x)=\rho(a^{-1}_{x,g_{0},\hat{g}_{0}}).

(6.107)

By the definition of the associator
a_{x,g_{0},\hat{g}_{0}}:(x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}g_{0})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\hat{g}_{0}\rightarrow x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(g_{0}\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}\hat{g}_{0})
in
[
MRW17
, (13)]
,
a_{x,g_{0},\hat{g}_{0}}

is the composition

\displaystyle a_{x,\alpha\cdot g_{0},\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}}:(x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot g_{0}))\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0})

\displaystyle=\iota(\xi(\iota(\xi(x)\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})))xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(x)\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})}\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}))}\iota(\xi(x)\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\iota(\xi(x)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0})})}\iota(\xi(x)\xi(\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}))))

\displaystyle=x\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}((\alpha\cdot g_{0})\mathbin{\circ_{\textup{h}}}(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0})).

Let
y
denote
\alpha^{-1}\cdot x
.
By the proof of Lemma
6.5
and the property of
\alpha\cdot\epsilon
and
\alpha\cdot\epsilon^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(x)\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})}\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}))

\displaystyle=\iota(\epsilon_{(\alpha\cdot\xi(y))(\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0}))}(\alpha\cdot\xi(\hat{g}_{0})))=\iota(\epsilon_{\alpha\cdot(\xi(y)\xi(g_{0}))}(\alpha\cdot\xi(\hat{g}_{0})))

\displaystyle=\iota((\alpha\cdot\epsilon_{\xi(y)\xi(g_{0})})(\alpha\cdot\xi(\hat{g}_{0})))=\iota(\alpha\cdot(\epsilon_{\xi(y)\xi(g_{0})}\xi(\hat{g}_{0})))=\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(y)\xi(g_{0})}\xi(\hat{g}_{0})),

\displaystyle\iota(\xi(x)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0})})

\displaystyle=\iota(\xi(\alpha\cdot y)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(\alpha\cdot g_{0})\xi(\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0})})=\iota((\alpha\cdot\xi(y))\epsilon^{-1}_{(\alpha\cdot\xi(g_{0}))(\alpha\cdot\xi(\hat{g}_{0}))})

\displaystyle=\iota((\alpha\cdot\xi(y))\epsilon^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot(\xi(g_{0})\xi(\hat{g}_{0}))})=\iota((\alpha\cdot\xi(y))(\alpha\cdot\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g_{0})\xi(\hat{g}_{0})}))

\displaystyle=\iota(\alpha\cdot(\xi(y)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g_{0})\xi(\hat{g}_{0})}))=\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(y)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g_{0})\xi(\hat{g}_{0})})

Thus, as the composition of

\alpha\cdot\iota(\epsilon_{\xi(y)\xi(g_{0})}\xi(\hat{g}_{0}))\mbox{ and }\alpha\cdot\iota(\xi(y)\epsilon^{-1}_{\xi(g_{0})\xi(\hat{g}_{0})}),

a_{x,\alpha\cdot g_{0},\alpha\cdot\hat{g}_{0}}
is the same functor as
\alpha\cdot a_{\alpha^{-1}\cdot x,g_{0},\hat{g}_{0}}.

In fact we have proved above the general conclusion that for any
g_{0,1}
,
g_{0,2}
,
g_{0,3}
in
QG
,

a_{\alpha\cdot g_{0,1},\alpha\cdot g_{0,2},\alpha\cdot g_{0,3}}=\alpha\cdot a_{g_{0,1},g_{0,2},g_{0,3}}.

(6.108)

Thus,

\alpha\cdot\psi_{(g_{0},\hat{g}_{0})}(\rho)=\rho(\alpha^{-1}\cdot a^{-1}_{\alpha\cdot-,g_{0},\hat{g}_{0}})

By the formula (
2.33
), we have

\alpha\cdot\psi_{\ast}:I^{\prime}=u^{\prime}(\ast)\to(\alpha\cdot f_{V},\alpha\cdot\eta_{v})(\ast)=f_{V,I},\quad(\alpha\cdot\psi)_{\ast}(\rho)(x)=\rho(r_{x})^{-1}=\psi_{\ast}(\rho)(x).

Note that
\alpha^{-1}\cdot I=I
.
Thus,

\alpha\cdot\psi_{\ast}=\psi_{\ast}.

As the Definition of
1-
morphisms in
2\textsf{Rep}S(QG)
,
T_{\alpha}
consists of the data below.

A morphism

in

•

A natural transformation

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces QG\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\alpha\cdot f_{V}}
\scriptstyle{f_{V}}

(6.109)

which is defined to be

\alpha:f_{V}\mapsto\alpha\cdot f_{V}

with

.

Thus, it’s straightforward to check that the diagram (

) (

) commutes. The vertical maps are all identity and the horizontal maps are the same.

Similarly, we have the
T_{\alpha^{-1}}:\alpha\cdot A\rightarrow\alpha^{-1}\cdot(\alpha\cdot A)=A
.

is a
1-
equivalence.

Proposition 6.14
.

and

by the adjoint action of
G
